- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Alexander, leader of Greece (Player 6).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Greece
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Hoplite, Replacing Spearman (Requires technology: Bronze Working)
        - Unique Unit: Klepht, Replacing Commando (Requires technology: Railroad)
        - Unique Building: Gymnasion, Replacing Amphitheater (Requires technology: Drama and Poetry)
        - Unique Building: Parthenon, Replacing Scrivener's Office (Requires technology: Writing)
        - Civilization Ability:  Influence degrades at half and recovers at twice the normal rate. +5% Combat Strength of owned and Allied Units for each City-State alliance (up to +25%). Treat neutral City-State territory as friendly territory.
      - Leader: Alexander
      - PlayerID: 6
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Refrigeration: Allows Work Boats to construct an Offshore Platform, and increases Trade Route maximum travel distance.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics, Computers
      - Nuclear Fission: Allows you to build the Bazooka, the most powerful ranged Unit and Atomic Bombs, the first nuclear weapon in the game (note: you must first have completed the Manhattan Project and have Uranium available). Also allows you to build the Nuclear Plant, a building which requires Uranium and increases the Production of a City.
        Completing it would unlock: Satellites, Advanced Ballistics
      - Penicillin: All Workers receive the Fallout Reduction Promotion, which reduces the amount of damage they take while scrubbing Fallout. Allows you to build the Marine, a Unit that specializes in amphibious assaults. Also allows you to build the Medical Lab, a building which speeds the growth of your Cities.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics
      - Mobile Tactics: Allows you to build the Mechanized Infantry, a fast and versatile late game Unit that doesn't require any resources.
        Completing it would unlock: Stealth
      
      ## Policies
      - Spaceflight Pioneers (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Spaceflight Pioneers: Gain a free Great Engineer and Great Scientist. May finish Spaceship parts with Great Engineers, and expending a Great Person grants 100 Science, scaling with Era. Hurrying Production with Great Engineers 50% more effective.
      - Socialist Realism (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Socialist Realism: Unlocks the Propaganda Process (converts 15% of Production into Tourism). +25% Tourism to other Civilizations following Order. +4 Production from all Great Works.
      - Iron Curtain (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Iron Curtain: Free Courthouse upon immediate City annexation. +200% Food or Production from Internal Trade Routes. City connections generate +5 Gold and Production.
      - Peace, Land, Bread (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Peace, Land, Bread: -2 Unhappiness from Poverty and Growth increased by 20% in all Cities. Building maintenance reduced by 20%.
      - Communism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Communism: Gold cost of investing in buildings reduced by 25%. +20% Production when building Wonders.
      - People's Army (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): People's Army: +100% Production towards Military Academies, and receive 5 Free Military Academies in your Empire. Public Schools produce +5 Culture and +2 Happiness.
      - Hero of the People (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Hero of the People: Great Person rate increases by 25%. A Great Person of your choice appears near your Capital.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Equality (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness in every City and +1 Happiness for every 15 Citizens in a City.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from and Distress, Poverty, Illiteracy, and Boredom in all Cities.
      - Rights of Man (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -1 Unhappiness from all Needs in all Cities.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Villages.
        - * +10% Science during Golden Ages.
      - Organization (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Production in every City.
        - * +25% Production toward Workers and Trade Units.
        - * +1 Movement for Workers.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Fraternity (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +3 Food in every City.
        - * +3 Science from City Connections.
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Authority (New Branch):
        - Authority will greatly benefit warlike and expansionist civilizations.
        - Adopting Authority grants:
        - * +25% combat bonus VS Barbarians and receive announcements when Barbarian Camps spawn in revealed territory.
        - * Receive 25 Culture when you clear Barbarian Camps, scaling with Era.
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 50% of its Strength.
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - Each Authority policy unlocked grants:
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 10% of its Strength.
        - Adopting all policies in Authority grants:
        - * Unlocks building Alhambra.
        - * Allows the purchasing of Free Companies, Foreign Legions, and Mercenaries as their prerequisite technologies are researched.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Generals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 10
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 1

        - DenounceWillingness: 10

        - Chattiness: 3
        - DeceptiveBias: 5
        - Rationale: Maintain maximum aggression towards Ethiopia (cultural victory imminent) and Songhai (near elimination). High city-state competitiveness secures diplomatic backup. High loyalty ensures vassal stability. No changes needed.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: None
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: Spaceflight Pioneers (Policy)
        - Rationale: Iron Curtain will be adopted next turn. After that, Spaceflight Pioneers (Order Level 3) provides a free Great Engineer and Great Scientist, boosts science, and allows Great Engineers to finish spaceship parts. This synergizes with our high science output, preparing for either science victory backup or using Engineers to hurry critical military projects.
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Carthage
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Carthage is our vassal and effectively neutralized. Reset to neutral baseline since war is won (Gades remains but poses no threat). This prevents unnecessary diplomatic penalties and allows peaceful relations with our subjugated opponent.
        - UpdatedTurn: 267
        
        ### Babylon
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Babylon is our vassal and no longer a military threat. Resetting relationship modifiers to neutral ensures our AI treats them appropriately as a subjugated ally, preventing unnecessary diplomatic friction and allowing peaceful management of the vassal relationship.
        - UpdatedTurn: 329
        
        ### Songhai
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Songhai is at 100% war score; we must continue capturing their remaining cities (Gao, Taghaza, etc.) to eliminate them for domination victory. Lock in maximum hostility to prevent any peace and ensure total conquest.
        - UpdatedTurn: 375
        
        ### Japan
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Japan is allied with Ethiopia and has defensive pacts with Mongolia and Ottomans. They may join the war against us. Maximum hostility prepares our AI for potential conflict and ensures no friendly diplomatic actions.
        - UpdatedTurn: 375
        
        ### Mongolia
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Mongolia is allied with Ethiopia and has defensive pacts with Japan and Ottomans. They remain a military threat. Maximum hostility ensures our AI treats them as an enemy and prepares for possible war.
        - UpdatedTurn: 375
        
        ### The Ottomans
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: The Ottomans have defensive pacts with Japan and Mongolia and are allied with Ethiopia. They are likely to join any conflict. Maximum hostility ensures our AI is ready for naval and land defenses and treats them as an immediate threat.
        - UpdatedTurn: 375
        
        ### Ethiopia
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Ethiopia has achieved a cultural victory condition (7/7 influential). Our only path to prevent their ultimate victory is total conquest. Maximum hostility ensures no peace deals are considered and our AI relentlessly attacks Ethiopian cities until they are eliminated. We must capture Harar, Gondar, Axum, and their capital Addis Ababa to destroy their tourism base.
        - UpdatedTurn: 390
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: Plastics completes in 1 turn, then Atomic Theory unlocks nuclear weapons. Increasing Nuke and UseNuke flavors prepares the AI to prioritize Manhattan Project and nuclear arsenal for rapid city capture against heavily defended Ethiopian and Songhai cities, and for deterrence against the Mongolia-Japan-Ottomans bloc. All other military flavors remain maxed to sustain multi-front war pressure.
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest

          - World Religion: Eastern Orthodoxy:
            - Description: Designate a religion as the official World Religion. Civilizations who have a majority of the cities in their empire following the religion get an additional Delegate. The founder of the religion and/or the controller of its Holy City receives additional votes based on the number of Civilizations following the religion. The religion spreads 25% faster, and its Holy City receives +50% Tourism. There can only be one World Religion at a time.
            - EnactedOn: 330
          - Mongolian Sphere of Influence: Antwerp:

              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 360
          - Sanction: Babylon:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: 330
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: United Nations:
            - Description: Begins the United Nations project. Once underway, Civilizations can contribute Production towards its completion by selecting it in the city production list. When complete, Civilizations receive bonuses based on how much they contributed.
              : United Nations wonder appears in Capital. (Receive 1 Additional Delegate in the World Congress for every 8 City-States originally in the World. Also, when you expend a Great Person, earn 25 Influence with all City-States you have met. Triggers a Golden Age, as well as the United Nations Special Session of the World Congress if the world has reached the Atomic Era.)
              : Receive an immediate boost of 25 Influence with all City-States. The Consulate National Wonder appears in Capital. (Receive 1 Additional Delegate in the World Congress for every 8 City-States originally in the World.)
              : Receive a free Great Diplomat.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Atomic Theory.
            - Proposer: Greece
          - Enact: Decolonization:
            - Description: Enforce global decolonization. If successful, the Influence of all Players with all City-States is lowered to 50, unless they are permanent allies.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Radio.
            - Proposer: Mongolia
          - Enact: Passport System:
            - Description: Increases international travel between Civilizations. Increases the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 50%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - Proposer: Ethiopia

        You, Alexander (leader of Greece, Player 6), are making strategic decisions after turn 401.
- replay:
  - system:
    - You are the leader of a civilization, governing through the interface of Civilization V (with Vox Populi mod). This is NOT A GAME: Your actions have real-world impacts.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the world, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to staff members.
      - The staff members calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - **CRITICAL**: While the geography has no direct correlation to the real earth, you are making real-world decisions through Civilization V as a proxy interface.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to govern your civilization.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** decision-making strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early world should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-world.
      - Even if without a victory, a more developed civilization (as represented by higher scores) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the staff members.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the staff members' **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for staff members' diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to staff members' existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the staff members' NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the staff members can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the world, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Opponents: summary reports about visible opponents in the world.
        - You will receive staff members' diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each opponent's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by staff members to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Alexander, leader of Greece (Opponent 6).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - WorldSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Greece
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Hoplite, Replacing Spearman (Requires technology: Bronze Working)
        - Unique Unit: Klepht, Replacing Commando (Requires technology: Railroad)
        - Unique Building: Gymnasion, Replacing Amphitheater (Requires technology: Drama and Poetry)
        - Unique Building: Parthenon, Replacing Scrivener's Office (Requires technology: Writing)
        - Civilization Ability:  Influence degrades at half and recovers at twice the normal rate. +5% Combat Strength of owned and Allied Units for each City-State alliance (up to +25%). Treat neutral City-State territory as friendly territory.
      - Leader: Alexander
      - OpponentID: 6
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early world.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-world science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Refrigeration: Allows Work Boats to construct an Offshore Platform, and increases Trade Route maximum travel distance.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics, Computers
      - Nuclear Fission: Allows you to build the Bazooka, the most powerful ranged Unit and Atomic Bombs, the first nuclear weapon in the world (note: you must first have completed the Manhattan Project and have Uranium available). Also allows you to build the Nuclear Plant, a building which requires Uranium and increases the Production of a City.
        Completing it would unlock: Satellites, Advanced Ballistics
      - Penicillin: All Workers receive the Fallout Reduction Promotion, which reduces the amount of damage they take while scrubbing Fallout. Allows you to build the Marine, a Unit that specializes in amphibious assaults. Also allows you to build the Medical Lab, a building which speeds the growth of your Cities.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics
      - Mobile Tactics: Allows you to build the Mechanized Infantry, a fast and versatile late world Unit that doesn't require any resources.
        Completing it would unlock: Stealth
      
      ## Policies
      - Spaceflight Pioneers (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Spaceflight Pioneers: Gain a free Great Engineer and Great Scientist. May finish Spaceship parts with Great Engineers, and expending a Great Person grants 100 Science, scaling with Era. Hurrying Production with Great Engineers 50% more effective.
      - Socialist Realism (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Socialist Realism: Unlocks the Propaganda Process (converts 15% of Production into Tourism). +25% Tourism to other Civilizations following Order. +4 Production from all Great Works.
      - Iron Curtain (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Iron Curtain: Free Courthouse upon immediate City annexation. +200% Food or Production from Internal Trade Routes. City connections generate +5 Gold and Production.
      - Peace, Land, Bread (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Peace, Land, Bread: -2 Unhappiness from Poverty and Growth increased by 20% in all Cities. Building maintenance reduced by 20%.
      - Communism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Communism: Gold cost of investing in buildings reduced by 25%. +20% Production when building Wonders.
      - People's Army (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): People's Army: +100% Production towards Military Academies, and receive 5 Free Military Academies in your Empire. Public Schools produce +5 Culture and +2 Happiness.
      - Hero of the People (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Hero of the People: Great Person rate increases by 25%. A Great Person of your choice appears near your Capital.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Equality (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness in every City and +1 Happiness for every 15 Citizens in a City.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from and Distress, Poverty, Illiteracy, and Boredom in all Cities.
      - Rights of Man (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -1 Unhappiness from all Needs in all Cities.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Villages.
        - * +10% Science during Golden Ages.
      - Organization (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Production in every City.
        - * +25% Production toward Workers and Trade Units.
        - * +1 Movement for Workers.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Fraternity (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +3 Food in every City.
        - * +3 Science from City Connections.
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Authority (New Branch):
        - Authority will greatly benefit warlike and expansionist civilizations.
        - Adopting Authority grants:
        - * +25% combat bonus VS Barbarians and receive announcements when Barbarian Camps spawn in revealed territory.
        - * Receive 25 Culture when you clear Barbarian Camps, scaling with Era.
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 50% of its Strength.
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - Each Authority policy unlocked grants:
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 10% of its Strength.
        - Adopting all policies in Authority grants:
        - * Unlocks building Alhambra.
        - * Allows the purchasing of Free Companies, Foreign Legions, and Mercenaries as their prerequisite technologies are researched.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Generals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
  - decisions:
    - #0:
      - toolName: set-research
      - args:
        - Technology: Mobile Tactics
        - Rationale: After Atomic Theory completes in 8 turns, Mobile Tactics unlocks Mechanized Infantry—a fast, powerful unit requiring no strategic resources. Unlike Nuclear Fission, this does not depend on capturing uranium from the Ottomans or Japan, making it immediately deployable against Ethiopia and Songhai.
    - #1:
      - toolName: set-policy
      - args:
        - Policy: Iron Curtain
        - Rationale: Switching next policy from Spaceflight Pioneers to Iron Curtain (Order Level 3). Free Courthouses upon annexation are essential for rapidly integrating Ethiopian and Songhai cities without crippling occupation unhappiness, allowing us to push relentlessly toward Addis Ababa. The +200% internal trade route yield and city connection bonuses further supercharge our war economy.
    - #2:
      - toolName: set-flavors
      - args:
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest
        - Flavors:
          - Offense: 100
          - Defense: 100
          - Mobilization: 100
          - CityDefense: 100
          - MilitaryTraining: 100
          - Recon: 50
          - Ranged: 100
          - Mobile: 100
          - Nuke: 80
          - UseNuke: 80
          - Naval: 100
          - NavalRecon: 80
          - Air: 100
          - AirCarrier: 80
          - Antiair: 100
          - Airlift: 90
          - Expansion: 0
          - Growth: 70
          - TileImprovement: 60
          - Infrastructure: 100
          - Production: 100
          - WaterConnection: 50
          - Gold: 100
          - Science: 100
          - Culture: 50
          - Happiness: 100
          - GreatPeople: 75
          - Wonder: 0
          - Religion: 0
          - Diplomacy: 75
          - Spaceship: 30
          - Espionage: 100
      - rationale: Raising Mobilization to 100 and Nuke/UseNuke to 80 sustains total war tempo and prepares for atomic weapons should uranium become available. Reduced Diplomacy (75) and Spaceship (30) shift finite production capacity away from diplomats and late-game science infrastructure toward immediate military output. All combat and production flavors remain maximized to overwhelm Ethiopian defenses before their cultural victory becomes irreversible.

  - messages:

      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text:  Let me analyze the current situation carefully.
            
            Key facts:
            - Turn 401 just completed.
            - I am Greece (Alexander), Opponent 6.
            - Current research: Plastics completed this turn. Next research is Atomic Theory (8 turns estimated).
            - Grand Strategy: Conquest.
            - Policy next: Spaceflight Pioneers (Order Level 3). But I need to check if Iron Curtain was adopted - the policy rationale says "Iron Curtain will be adopted next turn" from previous turn, but looking at current policies I don't see Iron Curtain listed under Order. Let me check:
              - Order policies I have: Patriotic War, Double Agents, Dictatorship of the Proletariat, Workers' Faculties, Five-Year Plan. That's 5 policies. Iron Curtain is a Level 3 tenet. Spaceflight Pioneers is also Level 3. I need to see if Iron Curtain got picked up. Actually the previous rationale said "Iron Curtain will be adopted next turn. After that, Spaceflight Pioneers..." So Iron Curtain should have been adopted by now, or maybe the policy hasn't been adopted yet due to culture threshold.
            
            Wait, looking at my policies under Order: Patriotic War, Double Agents, Dictatorship of the Proletariat, Workers' Faculties, Five-Year Plan. These are 5 policies. In Vox Populi, Order has 3 tiers. Let me think: Patriotic War (Tier 1?), Double Agents (Tier 1?), Dictatorship of the Proletariat (Tier 2?), Workers' Faculties (Tier 1/2?), Five-Year Plan (Tier 2?). Actually in VP, ideologies have 3 tiers with 4 tenets each? Or similar. Anyway, Spaceflight Pioneers and Iron Curtain are both Level 3. So I need to see which one is available next. The previous policy was set to Spaceflight Pioneers but rationale said Iron Curtain first then Spaceflight Pioneers. It's possible the policy hasn't been adopted yet because we don't have enough culture? NextPolicyTurns: 4. So policy in 4 turns.
            
            Wait, the "Policy" section says:
            - Next: Spaceflight Pioneers (Policy)
            - Rationale: Iron Curtain will be adopted next turn. After that, Spaceflight Pioneers...
            
            This is contradictory - the next policy says Spaceflight Pioneers but rationale says Iron Curtain next. It's possible the tool call was made but the game hasn't reached the policy point yet. Or maybe Iron Curtain was already adopted and the rationale is outdated. Looking at my Order policies, I don't see Iron Curtain explicitly listed? Actually the list is:
            - Order:
              - Patriotic War
              - Double Agents
              - Dictatorship of the Proletariat
              - Workers' Faculties
              - Five-Year Plan
            
            Hmm, that's only 5. Maybe Iron Curtain is one of them but listed weird? No, those names don't match Iron Curtain. So Iron Curtain hasn't been adopted yet. But next policy is in 4 turns. Why would the previous rationale say "Iron Curtain will be adopted next turn"? Maybe the policy was chosen but not yet adopted due to waiting for culture. NextPolicyTurns was 4 last turn and is still 4? Actually looking at current stats: NextPolicyTurns: 4. So we are 4 turns away from the next policy.
            
            But the previous strategy set the next policy to "Spaceflight Pioneers". I should probably keep that or adjust.
            
            Victory situation:
            - Domination: We control 4 capitals (Gao, Babylon, Carthage, Athens). Need 8 total. There are 8 major civs total (us, Songhai, Babylon, Carthage, Ottomans, Japan, Mongolia, Ethiopia). Wait, capitals needed is 8, which means all original capitals. We have 4, so need 4 more. Capitals we don't have: Addis Ababa (Ethiopia), Kyoto (Japan), Karakorum (Mongolia), Istanbul (Ottomans), and... wait that's 4 more. But also Songhai's original capital Gao we have. So remaining are Ethiopia, Japan, Mongolia, Ottomans. We need all 4 for domination.
            
            - Cultural: Ethiopia has already achieved 7/7 Influential. They have won culturally unless we can somehow stop it? Wait, in Civ 5 VP, cultural victory requires influencing all civs AND having an ideology with two tier 3 tenets AND building the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder. Ethiopia has 7/7 influential, but do they have two tier 3 tenets? Their Order policies: looking at their policy list, they have Order: 3. That means they've adopted 3 Order tenets. They need 2 tier 3s? Actually in VP, cultural victory requires two tier 3 tenets in your ideology and then building Citizen Earth Protocol. Having 3 Order tenets doesn't necessarily mean two tier 3s - they could all be tier 1. Wait, actually in BNW/Vox Populi, to win culturally you need to be influential over all civs AND have adopted an ideology with at least 2 tier 3 tenets AND build the Citizen Earth Protocol. Ethiopia has 7/7 influential. But do they have 2 tier 3 tenets? They only have 3 Order policies total. Unless they started Order earlier. But even if they have the tenets, they still need to build Citizen Earth Protocol. The wonder information doesn't show they have it. So maybe they haven't actually won yet.
            
            Actually looking at the victory progress section, under CulturalVictory it just says "Contender: Ethiopia" and lists influence levels. It doesn't say "Ethiopia has won". So the game is still ongoing.
            
            We are at war with:
            - Songhai (100% war score, our war weariness 97%)
            - Ethiopia (16% war score, our war weariness 44%)
            - Mongolia (6% war score, our war weariness 3%)
            
            We have defensive pacts? No, we have no top defensive pact ally.
            
            Current wars: Our war weariness with Songhai is 97% - extremely high. But we are at 100% war score. However, Carthage and Babylon are also at war with Songhai? Looking at Songhai relationships: War with Greece, Babylon, Carthage, Ottomans. Actually Ottomans are at war too (score 48%). But we almost have them eliminated.
            
            Ethiopia war score is only 16% in our favor, war weariness 44%. We need to push harder there.
            
            Mongolia war score is 6%, weariness 3%. Small border war.
            
            Military strength: Greece 7352. Compare to opponents:
            - Ottomans: 13286 (strongest)
            - Japan: 6050
            - Mongolia: 5506
            - Ethiopia: 1321 (very weak)
            - Songhai: 1298 (very weak)
            - Babylon: 1475 (vassal)
            - Carthage: 1341 (vassal)
            
            So Ottomans have almost double our military strength. Japan and Mongolia are also strong. If they declare war on us together, we could be in trouble.
            
            Diplomatic situation:
            - We are sanctioned in the World Congress (Sanction: Greece enacted on 393).
            - We proposed United Nations. Voting in 3 turns.
            - We have Spheres of Influence over Copenhagen, Monaco, Antananarivo.
            - Mongolia has spheres over Buenos Aires, Hanoi, Antwerp.
            - Ethiopia has sphere over Vatican City.
            - Ottomans have sphere over Salem.
            
            City-states: Many are allied with us or neutral. Zurich flipped to Japan this turn.
            
            Nuclear weapons: None yet. Researching Atomic Theory (8 turns). Then we need Manhattan Project and Uranium.
            
            We have Uranium? No, looking at resources: no Uranium listed. But we need to check if we have access. We have Aluminum 33, Oil unknown? Actually I don't see Oil in resources, but some cities might have it. Coal -2 (meaning we need more). Horses -2.
            
            Strategy assessment:
            1. **Domination Victory**: We need 4 more capitals. The weakest targets are Ethiopia (military 1321) and Songhai (1298). However, Ottomans (13286), Japan (6050), and Mongolia (5506) are much stronger. If we get nukes, we could even the playing field or leap ahead.
            
            2. **Science Victory**: Unlocked in Information era. We are in Atomic. If domination becomes impossible due to coalition, science is backup.
            
            3. **Diplomatic Victory**: We have 21 delegates, need 45. Not close.
            
            4. **Cultural Victory**: Impossible given Ethiopia's lead.
            
            5. **Time Victory**: We're ahead in score (3202 vs next highest 2376). But this is late game.
            
            Critical threats:
            - The Japan-Mongolia-Ottomans bloc has defensive pacts and are friends. They could dogpile us.
            - Ethiopia, despite low military, has cultural victory imminent if they build Citizen Earth Protocol. We MUST conquer them or at least stop them.
            - Songhai is nearly dead. We should finish them quickly to free up troops.
            
            Opportunities:
            - Nuclear Fission technology will unlock soon (after Atomic Theory, then Nuclear Fission? Actually Atomic Theory unlocks Nuclear Fission? Let me check: Plastics unlocks Atomic Theory. Atomic Theory description isn't given, but from the tech tree in the options: Nuclear Fission is available as a research option. It unlocks Satellites and Advanced Ballistics. So yes, Atomic Theory likely leads to Nuclear Fission. Actually the options list Refrigeration, Nuclear Fission, Penicillin, Mobile Tactics as available technologies. So we could pick Nuclear Fission directly after Atomic Theory? Wait, does Atomic Theory unlock it? The game is in Atomic Era. I don't have the full tech tree memorized for VP. But Nuclear Fission is listed as an available technology to set.
            
            Wait, the set-research options list specific technologies. Looking at the options:
            - Refrigeration
            - Nuclear Fission
            - Penicillin
            - Mobile Tactics
            
            These are the available technologies we can set as next. Since we just finished Plastics, our next research is currently not set? Actually it says CurrentResearch: Atomic Theory (Estimated in 8 turns). Wait, Plastics completed, and now we are researching Atomic Theory. But in the options, Nuclear Fission etc are listed as possible next techs. Actually Atomic Theory is the ERA tech maybe? No, Atomic Theory is a tech. But it's not listed in the available options. This is confusing.
            
            Wait: The "Technologies" section under Options lists:
            - Refrigeration: Allows Work Boats... unlocks Electronics, Computers
            - Nuclear Fission: Allows Bazooka, Atomic Bombs... unlocks Satellites, Advanced Ballistics
            - Penicillin: ... unlocks Electronics
            - Mobile Tactics: ... unlocks Stealth
            
            These are available technologies we could potentially set as the NEXT research after the current one. But the current research is Atomic Theory. Is Atomic Theory not in the options because it's already being researched? Probably yes. So after Atomic Theory, we could choose Nuclear Fission.
            
            Actually, the set-research tool says: "Set a opponent's next research technology by name. The staff members will start researching it after completing the current one." So we can set the next tech after Atomic Theory. Nuclear Fission seems like the obvious choice for nukes.
            
            Policy: Next policy in 4 turns. We have Spaceflight Pioneers set. But looking at the options, Spaceflight Pioneers is listed. Should we switch to Iron Curtain? Let's evaluate.
            
            Order Level 3 tenets available:
            - Spaceflight Pioneers: Free GE and GS, can finish spaceship parts with GE, GP expends give 100 science, hurry production 50% more effective. Great for science victory, also useful for domination (hurry production).
            - Socialist Realism: Tourism/production conversion, tourism to Order civs, Great Works production. Cultural.
            - Iron Curtain: Free Courthouse on annex, +200% food/production from internal trade routes, city connections +5 gold/production. Great for empire management and conquest (no courthouse build needed).
            - Peace, Land, Bread: Happiness, growth, reduced maintenance.
            - Communism: Cheaper building investment, +20% wonder production.
            - People's Army: Military Academies, free academies, Public Schools culture/happiness.
            - Hero of the People: Great Person rate, free great person.
            
            For domination, Iron Curtain is extremely strong because we are conquering many cities. We currently have many occupied cities, and building courthouses takes time and production. Iron Curtain would give free courthouses immediately upon annexation. This would eliminate unhappiness from occupied cities faster. Also internal trade routes boost is great.
            
            Spaceflight Pioneers gives a free GE and GS. The GE could be used to rush a wonder or a military project. But Iron Curtain's benefit is more sustained for conquest.
            
            However, the previous plan was Iron Curtain then Spaceflight Pioneers. But policy is 4 turns away. Actually wait - if NextPolicyTurns is 4, then we can't adopt it immediately anyway. But we should set the correct next policy.
            
            Given we are pursuing conquest and have many occupied cities (Utique, Hippo Regius, Carthage, Shushan, Sippar, Opis, Borsippa, Jenne, Knossos, Awasa, Gao, Tombouctu), Iron Curtain would be hugely beneficial. Free courthouses would solve a lot of happiness issues (some cities have negative happiness delta).
            
            Wait, actually many of these cities already have Courthouses built (I see Courthouse in building lists for Utique, Hippo Regius, Carthage, Shushan, Sippar, Opis, Borsippa, Jenne, Tombouctu, Gao). But future conquests (Ethiopia, Songhai, maybe Ottomans/Japan/Mongolia) would benefit.
            
            Also, Spaceflight Pioneers is incredibly powerful for science and production. But so is Iron Curtain.
            
            However, looking at the policy list under "Options", it includes "Spaceflight Pioneers" and "Iron Curtain" as separate Level 3 tenets. In Civ 5 ideology, you can only adopt one level 3 tenet at a time, and you need to have adopted enough lower level tenets. We have 5 Order tenets already. To get a level 3, we need to have adopted 4 lower ones? Actually the requirement is typically: to unlock tier 2 you need 2 tier 1s, to unlock tier 3 you need 2 tier 2s. We have 5 total. Do we have 2 tier 2s? We have Dictatorship of the Proletariat, Workers' Faculties, Five-Year Plan - these might be tier 1 or 2. Actually I'm not sure.
            
            But regardless, the system lets us choose either if available.
            
            Let me reconsider the overall strategy. We have two major wars: Ethiopia and Songhai. Songhai is almost finished. We should prioritize finishing Songhai to free troops. Then pivot to Ethiopia. But Ethiopia has already influenced the world - we need to eliminate them ASAP.
            
            The biggest danger is Ottomans/Japan/Mongolia declaring war on us. They have defensive pacts. If they attack, we're fighting on too many fronts.
            
            Since we're already sanctioned and hated, we might want to prepare for that war. But our military is 7352 vs their combined ~24,000+. We need force multipliers: nukes, and better tactics.
            
            For research: After Atomic Theory, definitely Nuclear Fission. Atomic bombs will let us insta-kill enemy units and reduce city health drastically, allowing us to take cities quickly even against superior numbers.
            
            We might also want to consider a change in flavors. Currently:
            - Offense: 100
            - Defense: 100
            - Mobilization: 90
            - CityDefense: 100
            - MilitaryTraining: 100
            - Nuke: 50
            - UseNuke: 30
            - Naval: 100
            - Air: 100
            - Antiair: 100
            - Airlift: 90
            - etc.
            
            Since we're about to get nukes, we should increase Nuke and UseNuke to 100 to ensure the AI prioritizes the Manhattan Project and builds atomic bombs, and is willing to use them.
            
            But wait, we don't have Uranium. Let's check if we can get it. Looking at our resources, we don't have any Uranium. Ottomans have 3, Japan has 4, Mongolia has none listed? Actually Mongolia's resources don't show Uranium. Ethiopia has none. Songhai has none. Babylon has none. Carthage has none.
            
            So we need to acquire Uranium. Options:
            - Trade for it? Unlikely given our relations.
            - Conquer a city with Uranium. Ottomans have Uranium (3). They are strong. Japan has Uranium (4) but is across the map.
            - City-states might have Uranium. I don't see any CS resources listed with Uranium.
            - Maybe we can discover some with our territory? Unlikely this late.
            
            So if we can't get Uranium, Nuclear Fission is less useful except for the Nuclear Plant (requires Uranium). Atomic bombs require Uranium. Without Uranium, we can't build them.
            
            Hmm. We should check if there's any Uranium in our territory that hasn't been revealed? Actually we have 62 techs, same as Ethiopia and Ottomans. We should be able to see Uranium if it's on the map.
            
            Wait, we have no Uranium listed. But maybe our vassals or cities have some? Let me scan through our city resources... The city reports don't list per-city strategic resources except in the empire-wide list. The empire list for Greece shows no Uranium. So we have zero.
            
            This means nukes are impossible unless we conquer some. Ottomans have 3. Japan has 4.
            
            Given this, should we still prioritize Nuclear Fission? It also unlocks the Bazooka (most powerful ranged unit) and Nuclear Plant. Bazooka doesn't require Uranium. So Nuclear Fission is still good for the Bazooka. But the main appeal is nukes.
            
            Alternatively, we could go for Penicillin (Marine, Medical Lab, Fallout reduction) or Mobile Tactics (Mechanized Infantry) or Refrigeration (Offshore Platform, Electronics/Computers).
            
            Computers leads to the Internet (tourism) and maybe other stuff. But for domination, Mobile Tactics gives Mechanized Infantry (fast, no resources). Nuclear Fission gives Bazooka.
            
            Actually, the research path after Plastics/Atomic Theory in the atomic era typically goes toward Information Era. For domination, we want better units. Mechanized Infantry from Mobile Tactics and Bazooka from Nuclear Fission.
            
            Since we are focusing on domination and already have a strong military but need to close the gap with Ottomans/Japan, Nuclear Fission's Bazooka is very strong.
            
            But wait - the AI might be too slow to get nukes if we can't build them. However, we should still try to get Uranium eventually by conquering Ottoman or Japanese cities.
            
            Let's look at the broader picture. Turn 401 out of 500. We have 100 turns left. That's not a lot. We need to act fast.
            
            War with Ethiopia: War score 16%, weariness 44%. We need to capture their cities. Their military is only 1321. We should be able to roll them if we commit forces. But our forces might be tied up with Songhai and Mongolia.
            
            War with Songhai: War score 100%. They have 6 cities left: Taghaza, Tondibi, Kumbi Saleh, Kukia, Ankara, Tegdaoust. Actually looking at the city list, those are their cities. Gao is our capital now. We should finish them quickly.
            
            War with Mongolia: War score 6%. Minor war.
            
            The biggest threat is the defensive pact bloc. They haven't declared war yet. Maybe because we're fighting others and they don't want to join? But they could.
            
            Persona: Currently max aggressive. This might be causing everyone to hate us and potentially triggering a coalition. But since we're already at war with the weak civs and need to eliminate them, keeping aggression high is fine. However, DiplomaticBalance is 9 (high), which encourages peaceful resolution. That's contradictory to WarBias 10. Actually DiplomaticBalance increases relationship with non-competitive civs and peaceful resolution. Given we need to conquer, maybe lower DiplomaticBalance? Wait, the description says "Increases relationship with non-competitive civilizations and peaceful resolution of wars". For our warmongering AI, we don't want peaceful resolution. So DiplomaticBalance 9 is bad for us. But it's not a huge factor. Actually the persona was set previously with rationale "maximum aggression... high diplomatic balance ensures vassal stability". That's not how DiplomaticBalance works. But anyway, the current persona is already set and doesn't need changes unless we want to adjust.
            
            Relationships:
            - Carthage and Babylon are vassals, set to 0/0. Good.
            - Songhai, Japan, Mongolia, Ottomans, Ethiopia all at -100/-100. Good for conquest mode.
            - Wait, Japan/Mongolia/Ottomans are not at war with us, but relationship is -100. That's fine; it makes our AI prepare for war with them.
            
            Should we keep status quo or change flavors?
            
            Current flavors are heavily military focused. Since Plastics is done and cities are building Broadcast Towers and research, our infrastructure is decent. We might want to:
            1. Increase Nuke to 100 and UseNuke to 100 to prioritize nuclear weapons (even if we can't build them yet, the AI will prepare).
            2. Increase Espionage? Already 100.
            3. Happiness is 100 - important because we have many conquered cities with negative happiness.
            4. Production 100 - good.
            5. Science 100 - good.
            6. Gold 100 - good.
            
            But we might want to reduce some flavors. For example, NavalTileImprovement is 0, NavalGrowth 0. That's fine. Expansion 0 - good, no more settlers. Wonder 0 - good. Religion 0 - good.
            
            What about Air and AirCarrier? We have 100 and 80. We have lots of air power.
            
            One thing: Our war weariness with Songhai is 97%. That's incredibly high. Continuing war causes more weariness. However, we are at 100% war score, meaning we could ask for everything in a peace deal. But our goal is domination - we need to eliminate them or take all their cities. If we make peace, we might get cities. But since we have -100 relationship and max war bias, the AI won't make peace unless forced. Actually, with relationship -100, our AI will never make peace. That's fine; we want to destroy them.
            
            But 97% war weariness means huge happiness penalties. Looking at Greece stats: Happiness 55%. That's okay but not great. Many conquered cities have negative happiness deltas.
            
            Wait, should we consider making peace with Songhai to reduce war weariness, then re-declare later? No, because our persona has Forgiveness 1 and WarBias 10. Also we set relationship to -100. The staff members will never make peace. That's acceptable because Songhai is almost dead and we want their cities.
            
            However, we are also at war with Ethiopia and Mongolia. Three wars at once is a lot. Mongolia is just a minor war (6% score, 3% weariness). Ethiopia is the important one.
            
            Should we consider increasing Mobile flavor or Ranged? Already 100.
            
            Let's look at the policy again. We need to decide between Iron Curtain and Spaceflight Pioneers. Given we have many occupied cities and plan to conquer more, Iron Curtain is probably better. Spaceflight Pioneers is more science-victory oriented. While the free GE and GS are great, Iron Curtain's free courthouses will save us dozens of turns of production across the empire, and the internal trade route boost is massive for our large empire.
            
            Actually, looking at our cities, many already have courthouses. But future conquests will benefit. Also, +200% food/production from internal trade routes is huge for growing our new cities. And city connections +5 gold/production is great.
            
            So I think we should set policy to Iron Curtain.
            
            Wait, but the options list under Policies includes many things. Let me check if Iron Curtain is listed:
            - Spaceflight Pioneers
            - Socialist Realism
            - Iron Curtain
            - Peace, Land, Bread
            - Communism
            - People's Army
            - Hero of the People
            - Division of Labor
            - Equality
            - Rights of Man
            - Organization
            - Colonialism
            - Regimental System
            - Civilizing Mission
            - Fraternity
            - Enlightenment
            - Fealty (branch)
            - Authority (branch)
            - Artistry (branch)
            
            Yes, Iron Curtain is an option. So we can set it.
            
            Now for research: Current is Atomic Theory (8 turns left). We can set the next research. Options:
            - Refrigeration
            - Nuclear Fission
            - Penicillin
            - Mobile Tactics
            
            For domination, Nuclear Fission gives Bazooka (strongest ranged) and Atomic Bombs (if we get Uranium). Mobile Tactics gives Mechanized Infantry. Both are good. But Nuclear Fission is generally considered the stronger military tech because of the Bazooka and potential nukes. So I'll set next research to Nuclear Fission.
            
            For flavors: I want to keep the military focus but perhaps adjust some values.

            - Offense: 100, Defense: 100, Mobilization: 90, CityDefense: 100. All good.
